Like I’ll know I should eat it, and I’ll know I usually like it, but I don’t feel hungry, and the whole thing feels like too much effort for something I don’t even want. So, no, gaining weight isn’t really the worry. It’s more that I just don’t want to eat.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really. I mean, when I do eat, it’s kind of the opposite, if anything. I’m not sitting there feeling like I can’t stop or like I’m eating way more than I meant to. It’s more like I’m trying to get myself to eat and I just don’t want it.\n\nLike, I’ll have food in front of me and I’ll sort of push it around or take a couple bites, and then I’m done with it. I don’t feel out of control exactly. I feel frustrated, I guess, because I know I should eat more than that, but it doesn’t feel like a binge or anything where I lose track and keep going. If anything, I have to kind of force the first few bites.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after eating?\n\nPatient: Um… guilty? Not really in the way I think you mean. Like, I don’t eat and then sit there thinking, “I shouldn’t have eaten that,” or feel bad because of calories or anything like that. That’s not really what happens.\n\nIf I feel bad after eating, it’s more… I don’t know, sometimes I feel annoyed with myself or weirdly uncomfortable, but not because I ate too much. More like I’ll think, “Why was that so hard?” or I’ll feel stupid that something as basic as eating a sandwich took so much effort. Or if I throw food away, I feel guilty about wasting it, because I paid for it and someone made it and all that. But after actually eating? No, I wouldn’t say I feel guilty for eating.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of eating in general?\n\nPatient: Afraid of eating in general… no, I don’t think afraid is the right word. It’s not like I’m scared something bad will happen if I eat, or like I’m nervous about food itself.\n\nIt’s more that I just barely want it anymore. That’s actually one of the big reasons I ended up asking for help, because it started feeling ridiculous, like… I’ll be on my lunch break at the store, and I’ll buy something I used to like, something normal that I know I would’ve eaten before, and then I just sit there staring at it. I’ll take a few bites, maybe, and then it’s like my body just says no. Not fear exactly—more like there’s no appetite there, no interest. And then I end up throwing most of it away, which makes me feel bad in a different way, but I still can’t really make myself eat it.\n\nSo, no, I wouldn’t say I’m afraid of eating. I just don’t want to eat much anymore, even when I know I probably should.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I have. That’s actually been one of the weirder things, because I used to have stuff I’d look forward to, even if it wasn’t anything huge.\n\nLike gallery nights — I used to get excited about those. I’d want to see what people were making, walk around, talk about it a little, even if I didn’t know what I was saying half the time. Or playing basketball, just shooting around, running a little, joking with whoever was there. And food-truck tacos, that sounds small, but that used to feel like a good part of the day, like something worth going out for.\n\nAnd I used to like helping kids with art stuff, too. That was one of the few things where I felt useful, like I could actually show them something or help them make something they were proud of. Lately, though, I’ll show up or try to go, and I just feel… flat. Like I’m standing there watching everyone else be into it, and I’m just not there with them.\n\nMost of the time I end up making some excuse to leave early, or I don’t go at all. I tell myself I’m tired or I’ve got something else to do, but really it’s just that the feeling isn’t there anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself feeling interested in those activities again, or has it been a consistent lack of interest?\n\nPatient: It’s pretty consistent, yeah. I mean, there are maybe tiny moments where I think, “Maybe this will be nice,” like if I hear there’s a gallery night or someone mentions going to shoot around, and for a second I remember how I used to feel about it. But then when I’m actually there, it just kind of drops out.\n\nLike with gallery nights, I used to actually want to look at everything and take my time, and now I’ll stand there and it’s like I’m pretending to be interested. Same with basketball — I used to enjoy just messing around, playing a little, being around people. Now I’ll go and feel flat almost immediately, like I’m just waiting for it to be over. Even food-truck tacos, which sounds dumb, but that used to be something I’d go out of my way for, and now it doesn’t really pull me in.\n\nAnd helping kids with art stuff… that one bothers me more, because I used to feel good doing that. Lately I’m there and I know I should care, or I remember that I used to care, but I mostly just feel blank. So I usually make some excuse to leave early, or I don’t go in the first place. It’s not like it comes back for a whole day or anything. It’s more like I remember being interested, but I don’t actually feel it much.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel sad or down when you're not interested in those activities anymore?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure how to separate those, honestly. Like, when I’m not interested, I don’t always think of it as “sad” right away. Sometimes it’s more like I’m just flat, or kind of empty, like I’m there but not really connecting to anything.\n\nBut then, yeah, sometimes after a while it does feel sad, because I notice it. Like I’ll be standing at some event or around people doing something I used to like, and I’ll realize I’m not feeling what I used to feel, and that makes me feel low. Not always in a crying way or anything dramatic, more like this heavy disappointment in myself, or this thought of, “What’s wrong with me? Why can’t I just enjoy this like before?”\n\nSo I guess… usually it’s numb first, and then sad after I think about it. But I don’t know if I’d say I’m sitting there feeling sad every single second. It’s more like the lack of interest is there, and the sadness kind of comes along with realizing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel sad or down most of the day?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. “Most of the day” is hard for me to measure, because I’m not always sitting there thinking, like, “I am sad right now.” A lot of the time it’s more like I’m just going through the motions and everything feels kind of muted.\n\nThere are parts of the day where I can get distracted or I’m just doing what I have to do, and I’m not necessarily naming it as sadness. But when I slow down, or when there’s nothing right in front of me to focus on, I do notice I feel pretty low. Not always crying or anything like that — more like heavy, worn out emotionally, like there isn’t much to look forward to.\n\nSo… yeah, I guess I do feel down a lot, but I’m not sure if it’s most of the day every day. It kind of depends on the day and how much I’m paying attention to it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’d say about two months now, give or take. It kind of started after that window display award at work… when the district manager made a big deal about it and there was all that attention on me.\n\nAt first I thought maybe I was just overwhelmed or embarrassed or something, because I’m not really used to people focusing on me like that. But then it didn’t really pass. Since then, it’s just been this steady thing where I feel low or blank a lot of the time, and stuff I used to care about doesn’t really feel the same.\n\nSo… yeah, around two months. It feels longer, honestly, but if I’m trying to place when it changed, that’s when I’d put it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or empty for most of every day over the past two months?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know if I can say “most of every day” in a precise way. It’s been there a lot, yeah — that low, empty feeling — but I don’t really track it hour by hour.\n\nSome days it feels like it’s just sitting in the background from when I get up until I go to sleep, and other days there are stretches where I’m busy enough that I don’t notice it as much. But then if I stop and actually check in with myself, it’s kind of still there, like under everything.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say it’s been happening a lot over the past couple months, but I’m not completely sure how to answer “most of every day” without feeling like I’m guessing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost pleasure in things you used to enjoy most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. That’s actually one of the things that feels really weird, because I can still technically do the same stuff, but it doesn’t land the same way.\n\nLike, I used to get excited about gallery nights — even small ones, where maybe it’s not a big deal, but I’d still want to go and look around and talk about what people made. Or playing basketball, even just casually, that used to clear my head a little. And I’d look forward to little things too, like grabbing tacos from the food truck, or helping kids with art stuff when that came up. Those things used to feel like… I don’t know, like little sparks in the week.\n\nLately, though, I’ll be there and it’s like I’m watching myself be there. I just kind of stand around feeling flat. People will be enjoying themselves or trying to talk to me, and I’m thinking I should be into this, I should want to stay, but I don’t really feel much. Most of the time I end up making some excuse to leave early, like I’m tired or I have something else to do, even when that’s not really the whole reason.\n\nSo, yeah. I wouldn’t say I hate those things now or anything. It’s more like the pleasure part got turned down really low.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad or empty most days?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think most days, probably. I keep getting stuck on the word “most,” because I don’t want to make it sound more exact than it is, like I’m keeping a chart or something. But if you asked me to describe the general shape of my days lately, it’s that — sad, or empty, or just kind of hollow.\n\nIt’s not always dramatic crying-sad. A lot of the time it’s quieter than that. Like I’ll just be sitting there or going through the day and there’s this blankness, like I’m not really connected to what’s happening. And then sometimes it turns into actual sadness, where I can feel it more directly, but other times it’s more like nothing feels right or warm or worth much.\n\nSo… yeah. Most days, I’d say there’s at least some part of the day where I feel that way, and on a lot of days it feels like it’s just there underneath everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past two months?\n\nPatient: I don’t know the exact number, honestly. I haven’t been weighing myself like that, so I don’t want to say “I lost this many pounds” and be making it up.\n\nBut my eating has definitely changed. Like… that’s actually part of what made me think I should ask for help, because I barely want to eat anymore. I’ll be on my lunch break at the store, and I’ll get something I used to like — something easy, just food I would normally eat without thinking — and then I’m just sitting there staring at it. I’ll take a few bites and it’s like my body just doesn’t want it, or I can’t make myself care enough to finish it, and then I end up throwing most of it away.\n\nAnd it’s not even always that the food tastes bad. It’s more like I look at it and feel nothing, or it feels like too much effort to keep eating. So… significant weight, I’m not sure. But appetite-wise, yeah, something’s been really off.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel depressed most days?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess that’s kind of what I was trying to say before, yeah. “Depressed” is a word I don’t always know how to use right, because sometimes I think of it as, like, being completely unable to function or crying all day, and that’s not exactly every single day for me.\n\nBut if you mean that low, heavy feeling, or that empty feeling where I’m just moving through the day and everything feels muted… then yeah, most days there’s some version of that. Some days it’s more obvious and I can really feel it sitting on me, and other days it’s more like background noise, like I’m not really myself but I’m still doing what I have to do.\n\nSo… yeah. I’d say most days, in some way, I feel depressed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest or pleasure in almost all activities most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s actually been one of the clearest things, because I can compare it to stuff I used to really look forward to.\n\nLike gallery nights — I used to get excited about those. Even if I didn’t know anybody there, I liked walking around, looking at the art, hearing people talk about it, feeling like there was something going on. Or basketball, even just playing casually, nothing serious. I used to enjoy that feeling of moving around and joking with people. And grabbing tacos from the food trucks — that sounds small, but that used to be something I’d actually look forward to, like a little bright spot in the day.\n\nAnd there’s this thing where I’d help kids with art stuff sometimes, and I used to feel good doing that. Like it made me feel useful, I guess, or connected to something. But lately when I’m there, or when someone invites me to something like that, I mostly just feel flat. I’ll show up sometimes, but I’m kind of just standing there, pretending I’m more into it than I am. And then I usually start thinking of an excuse to leave early.\n\nIt’s not that I hate those things now. That’s what’s weird. It’s more like the part of me that used to enjoy them just doesn’t turn on. So… yeah, most of the time, it feels like I’ve lost interest or pleasure in a lot of the things that used to matter to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been experiencing these low feelings for more than half of the days over the past two months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… more than half the days, definitely. I mean, if I’m being honest, it’s been kind of there for about two months now.\n\nIt started around when I got that window display award at work, and then the district manager started paying all this attention to it — to me, I guess — and I know that probably sounds like it should’ve been a good thing. Like, most people would just be happy about being noticed. But around that time is when everything started feeling different, and since then it’s been more days with that low feeling than without it.\n\nIt’s not like every single minute is exactly the same, but over these past two months, yeah… most days I’ve felt off or down in some way.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
